========================================
FILE: docs/module02/08_eval_data.md
========================================
# Phase 4: Evaluation Data Generation

> **Phase**: 4 of 6  
> **Scope**: Generate 4-layer evaluation dataset (golden, augmented, mutation, adversarial)  
> **Prerequisite**: Phases 1–3 complete  
> **Estimated Effort**: 2–3 days (includes running generators)  
> **Status**: Pending

---

## 1. Introduction

Module 02 requires a systematic, repeatable evaluation dataset to validate its correctness claims. Rather than relying solely on the IBM Flow-Bench dataset (which contains 101 test cases with proprietary API call patterns), we generate a **4-layer evaluation dataset** that provides:

1. **Fresh workflows** not seen during development (prevents overfitting)
2. **Known structural properties** (ground truth for V3 metrics)
3. **Known semantic mutations** (ground truth for V1/V2 detection rates)
4. **Stress-test edge cases** (boundary conditions for all components)

This document describes the complete methodology, implementation, and expected outputs.

---

## 2. Flow-Bench Methodology Analysis

### 2.1 Dataset Structure

The IBM Flow-Bench dataset [^19^] consists of 101 incremental test cases stored in `conditional_ootb.yaml`:

```yaml
tests:
  - _metadata:
      tags: ["1", "linear"]
      uid: 1
    input:
      utterance: "Create a new Jira issue and then create a github repository."
      prior_sequence: ""
      prior_context: []
    expected_output:
      sequence:
        - "issue = Jira_Issue__2_0_0__create_Issue()\nrepository = GitHub_Repository__3_0_0__create_Repository()"
      bpmn:
        - $ref: "output/uid_1_output.bpmn"
```

### 2.2 Taxonomy of Test Cases

From our analysis of the `tags` field across all 101 test cases:

| Tag Category | Count | Description | Example |
|-------------|-------|-------------|---------|
| `linear` | 34 | Sequential API calls | Create issue, then create repository |
| `conditional` | 19 | If/else on object properties | If priority=high, do X, else do Y |
| `conditional_update_replace` | 26 | Replace action in existing workflow | "Send email *instead of* slack" |
| `conditional_update_add` | 21 | Add branch to existing workflow | "Also add else clause" |
| `conditional_update_delete` | 14 | Remove action from workflow | "Remove the notification step" |
| `linear_update_add` | 11 | Add step to linear sequence | Append action |
| `linear_update_replace` | 6 | Replace step in linear sequence | Swap action |
| `linear_update_delete` | 5 | Remove step from linear sequence | Delete action |
| `user_task` | 16 | Include human approval step | `user_task("validate")` |

### 2.3 Key Insight: Incremental Build Pattern

Flow-Bench uses an **incremental build pattern** — complex test cases are generated by applying systematic transformations to simpler base workflows. This is the methodology we replicate.

```
Base:        "Create Jira issue, then create GitHub repository" (uid 1, linear)
                ↓ conditional_update_replace
Variant:     "Send email *instead of* creating repository" (uid 11)
                ↓ conditional_update_add
Variant:     "If urgent, send email *else* create repo" (uid 12)
                ↓ conditional_update_delete
Variant:     "Remove the email step" (uid 13)
```

---

## 3. Four-Layer Evaluation Strategy

```
Layer 1: GOLDEN WORKFLOWS         Layer 2: FLOW-BENCH DERIVATIVES
┌─────────────────────┐          ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ 50 fresh workflows  │          │ 100 augmented variants       │
│ GPT-4o-mini generated│    →    │ (add_guard, add_loop,       │
│ Not in Flow-Bench   │          │  fuse, invert_condition)     │
└─────────────────────┘          └──────────────────────────────┘
         │                                  │
         │    ┌─────────────────────────────┘
         │    │
         ▼    ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Layer 3: SEEDED BUG MUTANTS                  │
│ 100 mutations × 5 base workflows = 500 mutants│
│ (ROR, COR, BOR, STR, SVR operators)          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         │
         ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Layer 4: ADVERSARIAL STRUCTURES              │
│ 10 hand-crafted edge cases                   │
│ (dominator stress, deep nesting, walrus, etc)│
└──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## 4. Layer 1: Golden Workflow Generation

### 4.1 Purpose

Generate 50 fresh workflow specifications and their corresponding Python IR implementations using GPT-4o-mini. These workflows:
- Are **not present** in the IBM Flow-Bench dataset
- Cover all complexity levels (linear through deep_conditional)
- Use a simplified, generic API vocabulary (not IBM-specific)
- Have **known ground truth** properties (expected nodes, edges, decisions)

### 4.2 API Vocabulary

Instead of IBM-specific APIs (`Jira_Issue__2_0_0__create_Issue()`), we use a simplified vocabulary:

```python
# Generic workflow API vocabulary
API_VOCABULARY = {
    "crm": {
        "ops": ["create_customer", "update_customer", "delete_customer", 
                "find_customers", "create_order", "update_order"],
        "objects": ["Customer", "Order", "Lead", "Contact"],
        "properties": {
            "Customer": ["tier: str", "status: str", "credit_limit: int", "is_active: bool"],
            "Order": ["amount: int", "priority: str", "status: str"],
            "Lead": ["score: int", "source: str", "is_qualified: bool"],
        }
    },
    "support": {
        "ops": ["create_ticket", "update_ticket", "close_ticket", 
                "find_tickets", "assign_ticket", "escalate_ticket"],
        "objects": ["Ticket", "Agent", "Queue"],
        "properties": {
            "Ticket": ["priority: str", "severity: int", "category: str", "is_resolved: bool"],
            "Agent": ["workload: int", "skill_level: str", "is_available: bool"],
        }
    },
    "notification": {
        "ops": ["send_email", "send_sms", "send_push", "send_slack"],
        "objects": ["Message", "Template"],
        "properties": {
            "Message": ["urgency: str", "channel: str", "read: bool"],
        }
    },
    "storage": {
        "ops": ["create_file", "read_file", "delete_file", "list_files", "copy_file"],
        "objects": ["File", "Folder", "Document"],
        "properties": {
            "File": ["size: int", "type: str", "is_encrypted: bool"],
            "Document": ["version: int", "status: str", "requires_approval: bool"],
        }
    }
}
```

### 4.3 Prompt Engineering

The generation prompt is carefully structured to produce consistent, parseable output:

```python
GOLDEN_GENERATION_PROMPT = """You are a dataset generator for business process automation research.
Your task is to generate a realistic workflow specification and its Python implementation.

COMPLEXITY LEVEL: {complexity}

COMPLEXITY DESCRIPTIONS:
- "linear": Sequential API calls, no conditionals (3-6 lines)
- "conditional": 1-2 if/else branches checking object properties (5-10 lines)
- "loop": Single for-loop over a collection with body (5-8 lines)
- "conditional_loop": for-loop with if/else inside (8-15 lines)
- "nested_loop": 2-3 levels of nested for-loops (10-18 lines)
- "deep_conditional": 2-level nested if/elif/else chain (10-20 lines)

API VOCABULARY (use these operations and objects):
{api_vocab}

RULES FOR PYTHON CODE:
1. Function signature: def workflow_name(context: dict) -> str:
2. Use ONLY: assignments, if/elif/else, for-loops, comparisons
3. API calls: result = api_category_op_ObjectName()  e.g., customer = crm_create_customer()
4. Control variables must be properties checked in conditions
5. Return a status string describing the outcome
6. Include a docstring describing the workflow

OUTPUT FORMAT — Return valid JSON:
{{
  "uid": "golden_{idx}",
  "complexity": "{complexity}",
  "utterance": "<natural language workflow description>",
  "docstring": "<detailed description for validators>",
  "function_name": "<descriptive_name>",
  "control_variables": [
    {{"name": "var.prop", "type": "str|int|bool", "description": "..."}}
  ],
  "expected_nodes": <int>,
  "expected_edges": <int>,
  "expected_decisions": <int>,
  "python_ir": "def workflow_name(context: dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"...\"\"\"\n    ...",
  "api_calls": ["crm_create_Customer", ...]
}}

Generate ONE workflow of the specified complexity. Output ONLY valid JSON."""
```

### 4.4 Implementation

```python
# eval_data/generate_golden.py
import asyncio
import json
import os
from dataclasses import asdict
from typing import List
from openai import AsyncOpenAI

# Import API vocabulary
from .api_vocab import API_VOCABULARY

COMPLEXITY_LEVELS = [
    "linear",
    "conditional", 
    "loop",
    "conditional_loop",
    "nested_loop",
    "deep_conditional"
]


class GoldenWorkflowGenerator:
    """
    Generates fresh golden workflows using GPT-4o-mini.
    
    Produces workflows NOT in Flow-BENCH with known ground-truth properties.
    Each workflow includes the Python IR + expected structural metrics.
    
    Cost: ~50 workflows × ~1.5K tokens = ~$0.40 total
    """

    def __init__(self, api_key: str = None):
        self.client = AsyncOpenAI(
            api_key=api_key or os.environ["OPENAI_API_KEY"]
        )
        self.model = "gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18"

    async def generate_batch(
        self,
        count: int = 50,
        output_file: str = "eval_data/datasets/layer1_golden.json"
    ) -> List[dict]:
        """Generate N golden workflows distributed across complexity levels."""
        
        tasks = []
        for i in range(count):
            complexity = COMPLEXITY_LEVELS[i % len(COMPLEXITY_LEVELS)]
            tasks.append(self._generate_one(i, complexity))
        
        results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
        
        # Filter valid results
        workflows = []
        for r in results:
            if isinstance(r, dict) and "python_ir" in r:
                r["source"] = "synthetic_gpt4o_mini"
                r["layer"] = 1
                r["validated"] = False  # Set True after manual review
                workflows.append(r)
        
        # Save
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(output_file), exist_ok=True)
        with open(output_file, 'w') as f:
            json.dump(workflows, f, indent=2)
        
        print(f"[GoldenGenerator] Generated {len(workflows)}/{count} valid workflows")
        print(f"[GoldenGenerator] Saved to {output_file}")
        
        return workflows

    async def _generate_one(self, idx: int, complexity: str) -> dict:
        """Generate a single golden workflow."""
        
        # Select random API categories for variety
        import random
        categories = random.sample(list(API_VOCABULARY.keys()), 
                                   k=min(2, len(API_VOCABULARY)))
        api_text = "\n".join(
            f"{cat}: {', '.join(API_VOCABULARY[cat]['ops'])}"
            for cat in categories
        )
        
        prompt = GOLDEN_GENERATION_PROMPT.format(
            idx=idx,
            complexity=complexity,
            api_vocab=api_text
        )
        
        response = await self.client.chat.completions.create(
            model=self.model,
            messages=[{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
            temperature=0.7,
            response_format={"type": "json_object"},
            max_tokens=2000,
            seed=idx  # Reproducible
        )
        
        return json.loads(response.choices[0].message.content)

    def validate_workflow(self, workflow: dict) -> bool:
        """
        Basic validation of generated workflow:
        1. Python IR is syntactically valid
        2. Contains a function definition
        3. Returns a string
        4. Has at least one API call
        """
        try:
            import ast
            tree = ast.parse(workflow["python_ir"])
            
            # Check for function definition
            funcs = [n for n in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(n, ast.FunctionDef)]
            if not funcs:
                return False
            
            # Check for return statement
            returns = [n for n in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(n, ast.Return)]
            if not returns:
                return False
            
            return True
        except (SyntaxError, KeyError):
            return False


if __name__ == "__main__":
    gen = GoldenWorkflowGenerator()
    workflows = asyncio.run(gen.generate_batch(count=50))
```

### 4.5 Output Format

```json
[
  {
    "uid": "golden_0",
    "complexity": "linear",
    "utterance": "Create a new customer in CRM and send a welcome email",
    "docstring": "Creates a customer record and sends welcome email notification",
    "function_name": "onboard_customer",
    "control_variables": [],
    "expected_nodes": 4,
    "expected_edges": 3,
    "expected_decisions": 0,
    "python_ir": "def onboard_customer(context: dict) -> str:\n    ...",
    "api_calls": ["crm_create_Customer", "notification_send_email_Message"],
    "source": "synthetic_gpt4o_mini",
    "layer": 1,
    "validated": false
  },
  ...
]
```

---

## 5. Layer 2: FLOW-BENCH Derivatives (Augmented Variants)

### 5.1 Purpose

Take existing base workflows and apply **systematic transformations** to create variants with known structural properties. This tests whether Module 02's V3 extractor correctly handles:
- New guard conditions
- Added loop structures
- Fused sequential workflows
- Inverted condition sense

### 5.2 Augmentation Operators

```python
# eval_data/augment_flowbench.py

AUGMENTATION_REGISTRY = {
    "add_guard": {
        "description": "Add a new conditional branch checking an object property",
        "preserves_semantics": False,  # Extends behavior
        "prompt_template": """Given this Python workflow, add a new if/else branch 
that checks a property of an object and performs an additional API call in one branch.
The new condition should be realistic (check status, priority, size, etc.).
Return ONLY the modified code.

Original code:
{code}
"""
    },
    
    "add_loop": {
        "description": "Wrap API calls in a for-loop over a collection",
        "preserves_semantics": False,
        "prompt_template": """Given this Python workflow, wrap one or more API calls 
in a for-loop that iterates over a collection. The collection should be retrieved 
by a 'find' or 'list' operation that naturally precedes the loop body.
Return ONLY the modified code.

Original code:
{code}
"""
    },
    
    "invert_condition": {
        "description": "Invert all condition guards while swapping branches",
        "preserves_semantics": True,  # Behaviorally equivalent
        "prompt_template": """Given this Python workflow, invert the sense of ALL 
conditional guards (change == to !=, > to <=, 'if x:' to 'if not x:') AND swap 
the then/else branches to preserve the original semantics.
Return ONLY the modified code.

Original code:
{code}
"""
    },
    
    "fuse_sequential": {
        "description": "Concatenate two workflows sequentially",
        "preserves_semantics": False,
        "prompt_template": """Combine these TWO Python workflows into one sequential 
workflow. The second workflow should run after the first completes. 
Avoid variable name collisions by prefixing with 'w1_' and 'w2_'.
Return ONLY the fused code.

Workflow 1:
{code1}

Workflow 2:
{code2}
"""
    },
    
    "add_elif_chain": {
        "description": "Expand binary if/else into multi-way elif chain",
        "preserves_semantics": False,
        "prompt_template": """Given this Python workflow with a simple if/else, 
expand it into a multi-way elif chain with 3-4 branches checking different values 
of the same property (e.g., priority == 'critical', 'high', 'medium', 'low').
Return ONLY the modified code.

Original code:
{code}
"""
    }
}
```

### 5.3 Implementation

```python
class FlowBenchAugmenter:
    """
    Generates augmented variants of existing workflows.
    
    Uses GPT-4o-mini for structural transformations. Each transformation
    is applied to a base workflow to produce a variant with known properties.
    """

    def __init__(self):
        from openai import AsyncOpenAI
        import os
        self.client = AsyncOpenAI(api_key=os.environ["OPENAI_API_KEY"])
        self.model = "gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18"

    async def augment_workflow(
        self,
        base_workflow: dict,
        operator: str,
        second_workflow: dict = None
    ) -> dict:
        """
        Apply an augmentation operator to a base workflow.
        
        Args:
            base_workflow: The original workflow dict with 'python_ir' field
            operator: One of AUGMENTATION_REGISTRY keys
            second_workflow: Required only for 'fuse_sequential' operator
            
        Returns:
            Augmented workflow with metadata about the transformation
        """
        config = AUGMENTATION_REGISTRY[operator]
        
        # Build prompt
        if operator == "fuse_sequential":
            prompt = config["prompt_template"].format(
                code1=base_workflow["python_ir"],
                code2=second_workflow["python_ir"]
            )
        else:
            prompt = config["prompt_template"].format(
                code=base_workflow["python_ir"]
            )
        
        # Call LLM for transformation
        response = await self.client.chat.completions.create(
            model=self.model,
            messages=[{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
            temperature=0.2,  # Low — structural transforms need precision
            max_tokens=1500
        )
        
        new_code = response.choices[0].message.content.strip()
        new_code = self._strip_markdown(new_code)
        
        return {
            "uid": f"{base_workflow['uid']}_{operator}",
            "base_uid": base_workflow["uid"],
            "augmentation_type": operator,
            "preserves_semantics": config["preserves_semantics"],
            "original_code": base_workflow["python_ir"],
            "augmented_code": new_code,
            "layer": 2
        }

    async def generate_all_augmentations(
        self,
        base_workflows: List[dict],
        output_file: str = "eval_data/datasets/layer2_augmented.json"
    ) -> List[dict]:
        """Generate all augmentation variants for a set of base workflows."""
        all_variants = []
        
        for base in base_workflows:
            for operator in AUGMENTATION_REGISTRY:
                if operator == "fuse_sequential":
                    # Pair with a random other workflow
                    import random
                    other = random.choice([w for w in base_workflows 
                                          if w["uid"] != base["uid"]])
                    variant = await self.augment_workflow(base, operator, other)
                else:
                    variant = await self.augment_workflow(base, operator)
                all_variants.append(variant)
        
        # Save
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(output_file), exist_ok=True)
        with open(output_file, 'w') as f:
            json.dump(all_variants, f, indent=2)
        
        return all_variants

    @staticmethod
    def _strip_markdown(code: str) -> str:
        if code.startswith("```python"):
            code = code[9:]
        elif code.startswith("```"):
            code = code[3:]
        if code.endswith("```"):
            code = code[:-3]
        return code.strip()
```

---

## 6. Layer 3: Seeded Bug Mutants (Mutation Testing)

### 6.1 Purpose

Generate **syntactic mutations** of correct workflow code to validate that Module 02's V1/V2/V3 detects semantic alterations. This is the gold standard for evaluating verification effectiveness [^41^][^44^].

### 6.2 Mutation Operators

Based on standard mutation testing literature [^37^][^43^] adapted for Python workflow code:

| Operator | Code | Description | Semantic Effect | Detection Target |
|----------|------|-------------|----------------|------------------|
| **ROR** | `==` → `!=` | Relational Operator Replacement | Alters branch condition | V1 (trace divergence), V2 (guard CNF) |
| **AOR** | `+` → `-` | Arithmetic Operator Replacement | Alters computed values | V1 (state comparison) |
| **COR** | `and` → `or` | Conditional Operator Replacement | Alters compound guard | V2 (guard CNF change) |
| **COD** | `not x` → `x` | Conditional Operator Deletion | Inverts guard sense | V1, V2 |
| **BOR** | Swap if/else | Branch body swap | Preserves semantics | Should NOT be detected (equivalent) |
| **SVR** | `x.prop` → `y.prop` | Scalar Variable Replacement | Wrong variable in guard | V1 (wrong state), V2 (type error) |
| **STR** | Delete statement | Statement Removal | Missing API call | V1 (trace length), V3 (node count) |
| **SDI** | Duplicate statement | Statement Duplication | Extra API call | V1 (trace length), V3 (node count) |
| **RER** | Return value change | Return Expression Replacement | Wrong output | V1 (return value diff) |
| **JTD** | `break` → `continue` | Jump Target Destruction | Wrong loop exit | V1 (trace path), V3 (CFG edge) |

### 6.3 Implementation

```python
# eval_data/mutation_engine.py
import ast
import copy
import random
from typing import List, Dict, Optional, Tuple
from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass
class Mutant:
    """A single mutant with full metadata."""
    mutant_id: str
    operator: str
    original_code: str
    mutated_code: str
    is_semantic_altering: bool
    mutation_point: str  # Description of where mutation was applied
    
    # Ground truth for evaluation
    expected_detection: str  # "v1", "v2", "v3", "combined", or "none" (for equivalent)


class WorkflowMutator(ast.NodeTransformer):
    """
    AST-based mutation engine for Python workflow code.
    
    Applies one mutation per invocation. Multiple mutations to the same
    source require multiple mutator instances.
    
    Based on standard mutation testing operators from Ammann & Offutt (2008)
    and PITest implementation patterns.
    """

    def __init__(self, mutation_type: str, mutation_index: int = 0):
        self.mutation_type = mutation_type
        self.mutation_index = mutation_index
        self.applied_count = 0
        self.target_index = mutation_index  # Which occurrence to mutate

    # ─── ROR: Relational Operator Replacement ──────────────────────────
    
    def visit_Compare(self, node: ast.Compare) -> ast.AST:
        self.generic_visit(node)
        
        if self.mutation_type != "ROR":
            return node
        if not node.ops:
            return node
            
        # Map each operator to its replacements
        ROR_MAP = {
            ast.Eq: [ast.NotEq, ast.Lt, ast.Gt],
            ast.NotEq: [ast.Eq, ast.LtE, ast.GtE],
            ast.Lt: [ast.Gt, ast.LtE, ast.Eq],
            ast.LtE: [ast.Lt, ast.Eq, ast.GtE],
            ast.Gt: [ast.Lt, ast.GtE, ast.Eq],
            ast.GtE: [ast.Gt, ast.LtE, ast.Eq],
        }
        
        op_type = type(node.ops[0])
        if op_type not in ROR_MAP:
            return node
            
        if self.applied_count == self.target_index:
            # Apply mutation: replace with random alternative
            new_op_cls = random.choice(ROR_MAP[op_type])
            node.ops = [new_op_cls()]
            self.applied_count += 1
            return node
        else:
            self.applied_count += 1
            return node

    # ─── COR: Conditional Operator Replacement ─────────────────────────
    
    def visit_BoolOp(self, node: ast.BoolOp) -> ast.AST:
        self.generic_visit(node)
        
        if self.mutation_type != "COR":
            return node
            
        op_type = type(node.op)
        if op_type == ast.And:
            if self.applied_count == self.target_index:
                node.op = ast.Or()
                self.applied_count += 1
            else:
                self.applied_count += 1
        elif op_type == ast.Or:
            if self.applied_count == self.target_index:
                node.op = ast.And()
                self.applied_count += 1
            else:
                self.applied_count += 1
                
        return node

    # ─── BOR: Branch body swap ────────────────────────────────────────
    
    def visit_If(self, node: ast.If) -> ast.AST:
        self.generic_visit(node)
        
        if self.mutation_type != "BOR":
            return node
            
        if not node.orelse:
            return node  # No else to swap
            
        if self.applied_count == self.target_index:
            node.body, node.orelse = node.orelse, node.body
            self.applied_count += 1
        else:
            self.applied_count += 1
            
        return node

    # ─── STR: Statement Removal ───────────────────────────────────────
    
    def visit_Assign(self, node: ast.Assign) -> ast.AST:
        self.generic_visit(node)
        
        if self.mutation_type != "STR":
            return node
            
        if self.applied_count == self.target_index:
            # Return a Pass node (no-op) instead of the assignment
            self.applied_count += 1
            return ast.Pass()
        else:
            self.applied_count += 1
            return node

    # ─── SDI: Statement Duplication ───────────────────────────────────
    
    # Handled at the statement-list level in visit_FunctionDef
    
    # ─── JTD: Jump Target Destruction ─────────────────────────────────
    
    def visit_Break(self, node: ast.Break) -> ast.AST:
        if self.mutation_type == "JTD":
            if self.applied_count == self.target_index:
                self.applied_count += 1
                return ast.Continue()
            self.applied_count += 1
        return node
    
    def visit_Continue(self, node: ast.Continue) -> ast.AST:
        if self.mutation_type == "JTD":
            if self.applied_count == self.target_index:
                self.applied_count += 1
                return ast.Break()
            self.applied_count += 1
        return node

    # ─── RER: Return Expression Replacement ───────────────────────────
    
    def visit_Return(self, node: ast.Return) -> ast.AST:
        self.generic_visit(node)
        
        if self.mutation_type != "RER":
            return node
            
        if node.value and isinstance(node.value, ast.Constant):
            if self.applied_count == self.target_index:
                # Mutate string return value
                if isinstance(node.value.value, str):
                    node.value.value = f"mutated_{node.value.value}"
                    self.applied_count += 1
            else:
                self.applied_count += 1
                
        return node


def apply_mutation(source_code: str, mutation_type: str, index: int = 0) -> Tuple[str, bool]:
    """
    Apply a single mutation to workflow code.
    
    Returns:
        (mutated_code, was_applied): The mutated code and whether the mutation
        was actually applied (some operators may not be applicable to all code)
    """
    tree = ast.parse(source_code)
    mutator = WorkflowMutator(mutation_type, index)
    mutated_tree = mutator.visit(tree)
    ast.fix_missing_locations(mutated_tree)
    
    was_applied = mutator.applied_count > 0
    
    try:
        mutated_code = ast.unparse(mutated_tree)
    except Exception:
        return source_code, False
    
    return mutated_code, was_applied


def generate_mutants(
    source_code: str,
    n_per_type: int = 20,
    base_uid: str = "unknown"
) -> List[Mutant]:
    """
    Generate all mutants for a given workflow.
    
    Produces up to n_per_type × 8 operator types = ~160 mutants.
    Filters out mutations that couldn't be applied.
    
    Args:
        source_code: The original (correct) Python workflow code
        n_per_type: How many mutations of each type to attempt
        base_uid: Identifier for the base workflow
        
    Returns:
        List of successfully applied Mutant objects
    """
    operators = {
        "ROR": {"semantic": True, "detect_by": "combined"},
        "COR": {"semantic": True, "detect_by": "v2"},
        "BOR": {"semantic": False, "detect_by": "none"},  # Equivalent!
        "STR": {"semantic": True, "detect_by": "v1"},
        "JTD": {"semantic": True, "detect_by": "v1"},
        "RER": {"semantic": True, "detect_by": "v1"},
    }
    
    all_mutants = []
    
    for op_type, config in operators.items():
        for i in range(n_per_type):
            mutated, applied = apply_mutation(source_code, op_type, i)
            
            if applied and mutated != source_code:
                all_mutants.append(Mutant(
                    mutant_id=f"{base_uid}_{op_type}_{i}",
                    operator=op_type,
                    original_code=source_code,
                    mutated_code=mutated,
                    is_semantic_altering=config["semantic"],
                    mutation_point=f"{op_type} at occurrence {i}",
                    expected_detection=config["detect_by"]
                ))
    
    return all_mutants
```

### 6.4 Mutation Score Calculation

```python
def calculate_mutation_score(mutants: List[Mutant], results: Dict[str, dict]) -> dict:
    """
    Calculate mutation testing metrics from verification results.
    
    Args:
        mutants: List of all generated mutants
        results: Dict mapping mutant_id → certificate from Module 02
        
    Returns:
        Metrics dict with detection rates
    """
    semantic_mutants = [m for m in mutants if m.is_semantic_altering]
    equivalent_mutants = [m for m in mutants if not m.is_semantic_altering]
    
    # Detection: combined < 0.95 means "caught"
    semantic_detected = sum(
        1 for m in semantic_mutants 
        if results.get(m.mutant_id, {}).get("combined", 1.0) < 0.95
    )
    
    # False positives: equivalent mutants wrongly rejected
    equivalent_rejected = sum(
        1 for m in equivalent_mutants
        if results.get(m.mutant_id, {}).get("combined", 1.0) < 0.95
    )
    
    return {
        "total_mutants": len(mutants),
        "semantic_mutants": len(semantic_mutants),
        "equivalent_mutants": len(equivalent_mutants),
        "semantic_detected": semantic_detected,
        "equivalent_rejected": equivalent_rejected,
        "detection_rate": semantic_detected / len(semantic_mutants) if semantic_mutants else 0,
        "false_positive_rate": equivalent_rejected / len(equivalent_mutants) if equivalent_mutants else 0,
        "target_detection": 0.95,  # 95% target
        "target_false_positive": 0.05  # 5% target
    }
```

---

## 7. Layer 4: Adversarial Structures

### 7.1 Purpose

Hand-crafted edge cases that stress-test specific components of Module 02:

| Case | UID | Target Component | Stress Factor |
|------|-----|-----------------|---------------|
| Diamond CFG pattern | `adv_001` | `DominatorAnalyzer` | Multiple paths converging |
| 4-level elif chain | `adv_002` | `GuardExtractor.guard_to_cnf` | Deep guard nesting |
| Break in nested loops | `adv_003` | `CFGExtractor._handle_Break` | Loop exit edges |
| Walrus operator guard | `adv_004` | `GuardExtractor.visit_NamedExpr` | Python 3.8+ feature |
| Empty else branch | `adv_005` | `GuardExtractor` | Missing else body |
| Complex boolean guard | `adv_006` | `GuardExtractor.flatten_to_cnf` | Multi-clause CNF |
| While-true with break | `adv_007` | `CFGExtractor._handle_While` | Loop boundary detection |
| Nested try-except | `adv_008` | `CFGExtractor._handle_Try` | Exception edges |
| Match statement (Py3.10) | `adv_009` | `CFGExtractor._handle_Match` | Structural pattern matching |
| Async/await workflow | `adv_010` | Full pipeline | Async control flow |

### 7.2 Implementation

```python
# eval_data/adversarial_workflows.py

ADVERSARIAL_CASES = [
    {
        "uid": "adv_001",
        "name": "Diamond CFG: converging paths",
        "target_component": "DominatorAnalyzer.immediate_dominators",
        "stress_factor": "Multiple paths merge — dominator tree must correctly identify merge nodes",
        "python_ir": '''
def diamond_workflow(incident: dict) -> str:
    """Diamond pattern: two independent checks converge."""
    ticket = support_create_ticket()
    if incident.get("priority") == "high":
        if incident.get("severity") > 5:
            support_escalate_ticket()
    if incident.get("priority") == "high":  # Second check — merge point stress
        notification_send_email()
    return "processed"
''',
        "expected_nodes": 9,
        "expected_edges": 11,
        "expected_decisions": 4
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_002",
        "name": "Deep elif chain: 4-way priority routing",
        "target_component": "GuardExtractor.guard_to_cnf",
        "stress_factor": "4-level elif → 4 CNF clauses with mutual exclusion",
        "python_ir": '''
def route_ticket(ticket: dict) -> str:
    """Route ticket based on 4-level priority."""
    if ticket.get("priority") == "critical":
        support_escalate_ticket()
        result = "escalated"
    elif ticket.get("priority") == "high":
        support_assign_ticket()
        result = "assigned"
    elif ticket.get("priority") == "medium":
        notification_send_slack()
        result = "notified"
    else:
        crm_create_customer()
        result = "logged"
    return result
''',
        "expected_nodes": 11,
        "expected_edges": 12,
        "expected_decisions": 4
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_003",
        "name": "Break in nested loops with early exit",
        "target_component": "CFGExtractor._handle_Break",
        "stress_factor": "Nested loop + conditional break creates complex loop exit edges",
        "python_ir": '''
def find_and_alert() -> str:
    """Search through customers and orders with early exit."""
    customers = crm_find_customers()
    for customer in customers:
        orders = crm_find_orders()
        for order in orders:
            if order.get("amount", 0) > 10000:
                notification_send_email()
                break  # Exit inner loop
        if customer.get("tier") == "vip":
            support_create_ticket()
            break  # Exit outer loop
    return "processed"
''',
        "expected_nodes": 14,
        "expected_edges": 17,
        "expected_decisions": 5,
        "expected_loop_depth": 2
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_004",
        "name": "Walrus operator in guard condition",
        "target_component": "GuardExtractor.visit_NamedExpr",
        "stress_factor": "Named expression (:=) creates implicit assignment in guard",
        "python_ir": '''
def walrus_guard(context: dict) -> str:
    """Walrus operator: assignment inside condition."""
    if (customer := crm_find_customers()) and customer.get("is_active"):
        crm_update_customer()
        return "active_customer_found"
    return "no_active_customer"
''',
        "expected_nodes": 7,
        "expected_edges": 7,
        "expected_decisions": 2
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_005",
        "name": "Empty else branch (pass-only)",
        "target_component": "GuardExtractor.flatten_to_cnf",
        "stress_factor": "Empty else body must not create false edges in CFG",
        "python_ir": '''
def conditional_with_empty_else(ticket: dict) -> str:
    """If with no else action — should not create spurious edges."""
    if ticket.get("priority") == "high":
        support_escalate_ticket()
    else:
        pass  # Explicit empty else
    return "checked"
''',
        "expected_nodes": 5,
        "expected_edges": 5,
        "expected_decisions": 2
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_006",
        "name": "Complex boolean guard: 4-clause CNF",
        "target_component": "GuardExtractor.flatten_to_cnf",
        "stress_factor": "Compound boolean with and/or/mix → complex CNF transformation",
        "python_ir": '''
def complex_guard(order: dict, customer: dict) -> str:
    """Complex compound guard requiring full De Morgan application."""
    if (order.get("amount", 0) > 5000 and customer.get("tier") == "gold") or \\\\n       (order.get("amount", 0) > 10000 and not customer.get("is_blocked", False)):
        crm_create_order()
        notification_send_email()
        return "premium_order"
    return "standard_order"
''',
        "expected_nodes": 8,
        "expected_edges": 9,
        "expected_decisions": 3
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_007",
        "name": "While-true with conditional break",
        "target_component": "CFGExtractor._handle_While",
        "stress_factor": "Infinite loop with break — loop boundary detection",
        "python_ir': '''
def polling_loop(queue: dict) -> str:
    """Poll queue until condition met — while-true pattern."""
    attempts = 0
    while True:
        item = storage_read_file()
        if item is None or attempts > 10:
            break
        if item.get("status") == "ready":
            support_create_ticket()
            break
        attempts += 1
    return "polling_complete"
''',
        "expected_nodes": 10,
        "expected_edges": 12,
        "expected_decisions": 4
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_008",
        "name": "Try-except with multiple handlers",
        "target_component": "CFGExtractor._handle_Try",
        "stress_factor": "Exception edges create alternative control flow paths",
        "python_ir": '''
def safe_api_call(context: dict) -> str:
    """API call with exception handling for different error types."""
    try:
        result = crm_create_customer()
        if result.get("duplicate"):
            raise ValueError("duplicate")
        return "created"
    except ValueError:
        crm_update_customer()
        return "updated"
    except Exception:
        support_create_ticket()
        return "failed"
''',
        "expected_nodes": 10,
        "expected_edges": 12,
        "expected_decisions": 3
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_009",
        "name": "Structural pattern matching (match/case)",
        "target_component": "CFGExtractor._handle_Match",
        "stress_factor": "Python 3.10 match → multi-way dispatch, variable binding",
        "python_ir": '''
def route_by_status(order: dict) -> str:
    """Route order using structural pattern matching."""
    match order.get("status"):
        case "pending":
            notification_send_email()
            result = "notified"
        case "approved":
            crm_create_order()
            result = "created"
        case "rejected":
            support_create_ticket()
            result = "ticketed"
        case _:
            result = "unknown"
    return result
''',
        "expected_nodes": 10,
        "expected_edges": 11,
        "expected_decisions": 4
    },
    {
        "uid": "adv_010",
        "name": "For-else pattern (completion detection)",
        "target_component": "CFGExtractor._handle_For",
        "stress_factor": "for-else: else runs only if loop completed without break",
        "python_ir": '''
def search_with_fallback(items: list) -> str:
    """Search list with fallback if not found."""
    for item in items:
        if item.get("id") == "target":
            crm_update_customer()
            result = "found"
            break
    else:
        crm_create_customer()
        result = "created_fallback"
    return result
''',
        "expected_nodes": 8,
        "expected_edges": 9,
        "expected_decisions": 3
    }
]
```

## 8. Complete Dataset Structure

```
eval_data/
├── datasets/
│   ├── layer1_golden.json           # 50 fresh workflows
│   ├── layer2_augmented.json        # 100 augmented variants
│   ├── layer3_mutants.json          # 500 seeded bug mutants
│   └── layer4_adversarial.json      # 10 hand-crafted edge cases
├── api_vocab.py                     # Generic API vocabulary

├── augment_flowbench.py             # Layer 2 augmenter
├── mutation_engine.py               # Layer 3 mutation engine
├── adversarial_workflows.py         # Layer 4 adversarial cases

└── README.md                        # Dataset documentation
```

# Phase 5: Experiments & Metric Calibration

> **Phase**: 5 of 6  
> **Scope**: Run seeded bug detection, calibrate thresholds, measure all target metrics  
> **Prerequisite**: Phases 1–4 complete (core hardened, AI refinement integrated, evaluation data generated)  
> **Estimated Effort**: 2–3 days (mostly automated, includes analysis)  
> **Status**: Pending

---

## 1. Experimental Objectives

This phase produces the **empirical evidence** that Module 02 works as designed. We run three controlled experiments:

| Experiment | Dataset | Purpose | Success Criteria |
|-----------|---------|---------|-----------------|
| **E1: Seeded Bug Detection** | Layer 3 (500 mutants) | Validate that V1+V2+V3 catches semantic alterations | ≥95% detection rate, ≤5% false positive |
| **E2: Structural Accuracy** | Layer 1 + Layer 4 (60 workflows) | Validate V3 extraction correctness | ≥98% node/edge match rate |
| **E3: Confidence Calibration** | Layer 1 + Layer 2 (150 workflows) | Validate that combined ≥ 0.95 correlates with correctness | Pearson r ≥ 0.85 between combined score and ground truth |

---

## 2. Experiment E1: Seeded Bug Detection

### 2.1 Protocol

```
For each base workflow in Layer 1 (5 selected workflows):
    1. Generate 100 mutants (20 per operator type: ROR, COR, BOR, STR, JTD)
    2. Run each mutant through Module 02 /verify endpoint
    3. Record certificate for each mutant
    4. Classify:
        - DETECTED: combined < 0.95  (verification caught the bug)
        - UNDETECTED: combined >= 0.95 (verification missed the bug)
    5. Separate semantic-altering from equivalent mutants
    
Calculate metrics:
    - Detection Rate = detected_semantic / total_semantic
    - False Positive Rate = rejected_equivalent / total_equivalent
```

### 2.2 Implementation

```python
# eval_data/run_experiments.py — Experiment E1

async def experiment_e1_seed_detection(
    module02_endpoint: str,
    base_workflows: List[dict],
    n_mutations_per_type: int = 20,
    output_file: str = "eval_data/results/e1_seed_detection.json"
) -> dict:
    """
    Seeded bug detection experiment.
    
    Validates that Module 02 detects semantic-altering mutations
    while accepting semantically-equivalent mutations.
    
    Returns:
        Metrics dict with detection_rate, false_positive_rate,
        per-operator breakdown, and per-mode detection rates.
    """
    from eval_data.mutation_engine import generate_mutants, calculate_mutation_score
    
    all_mutants = []
    all_results = {}
    
    for base in base_workflows:
        # Generate mutants for this base workflow
        mutants = generate_mutants(
            source_code=base["python_ir"],
            n_per_type=n_mutations_per_type,
            base_uid=base["uid"]
        )
        all_mutants.extend(mutants)
        
        # Run each mutant through Module 02
        for mutant in mutants:
            result = await _call_verify(module02_endpoint, mutant.mutated_code)
            all_results[mutant.mutant_id] = result
    
    # Calculate metrics
    metrics = calculate_mutation_score(all_mutants, all_results)
    
    # Per-operator breakdown
    per_operator = {}
    for op in ["ROR", "COR", "BOR", "STR", "JTD", "RER"]:
        op_mutants = [m for m in all_mutants if m.operator == op]
        op_semantic = [m for m in op_mutants if m.is_semantic_altering]
        op_detected = sum(
            1 for m in op_semantic
            if all_results.get(m.mutant_id, {}).get("combined", 1.0) < 0.95
        )
        per_operator[op] = {
            "total": len(op_mutants),
            "semantic": len(op_semantic),
            "detected": op_detected,
            "detection_rate": op_detected / len(op_semantic) if op_semantic else 0
        }
    
    # Per-mode detection rates
    per_mode = {}
    for mode in ["v1", "v2", "v3"]:
        mode_detected = sum(
            1 for m in all_mutants
            if m.is_semantic_altering 
            and all_results.get(m.mutant_id, {}).get(mode, {}).get("score", 0) < 0.5
        )
        per_mode[mode] = mode_detected / len([m for m in all_mutants if m.is_semantic_altering])
    
    result = {
        "experiment": "E1_Seeded_Bug_Detection",
        "total_mutants": len(all_mutants),
        "base_workflows_used": len(base_workflows),
        "mutations_per_type": n_mutations_per_type,
        "overall_detection_rate": metrics["detection_rate"],
        "overall_false_positive_rate": metrics["false_positive_rate"],
        "target_detection_rate": 0.95,
        "target_false_positive_rate": 0.05,
        "detection_target_met": metrics["detection_rate"] >= 0.95,
        "fp_target_met": metrics["false_positive_rate"] <= 0.05,
        "per_operator": per_operator,
        "per_mode": per_mode,
        "raw_results": all_results,
        "timestamp": datetime.utcnow().isoformat()
    }
    
    # Save
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(output_file), exist_ok=True)
    with open(output_file, 'w') as f:
        json.dump(result, f, indent=2)
    
    return result
```

### 2.3 Expected Results

| Metric | Target | Expected | Interpretation |
|--------|--------|----------|----------------|
| Semantic mutation detection rate | ≥95% | 96–99% | V1 differential testing catches most behavioral changes |
| Equivalent mutant false positive rate | ≤5% | 3–8% | BOR (branch swap) may occasionally trigger V1 due to trace ordering |
| ROR detection | — | ~98% | Relational operator changes are obvious in both V1 and V2 |
| COR detection | — | ~95% | Conditional operator changes detected by V2 guard CNF |
| BOR (equivalent) rejection | — | ~5% | Branch swap preserves semantics but may trigger V1 stutter detection |
| STR detection | — | ~99% | Statement removal always detected by V1 trace length difference |

### 2.4 Analysis Procedure

If detection rate < 95%:
1. Identify which operators are under-detected
2. Check if V1 test runs (n=50) are sufficient — increase to 100
3. Check if V2 solver budget (50 queries) is exhausted — increase to 100
4. If BOR false positives > 5%: tune V1 stutter elimination threshold

---

## 3. Experiment E2: Structural Accuracy

### 3.1 Protocol

```
For each workflow in Layer 1 (50 golden) + Layer 4 (10 adversarial):
    1. Run through Module 02 /verify endpoint
    2. Extract V3 structural metrics (nodes, edges, decisions)
    3. Compare against ground-truth expected values
    4. Classify:
        - MATCH: All structural metrics within ±1 of expected
        - MISMATCH: Any metric differs by >1
        - FAIL: Verification threw exception
```

### 3.2 Ground Truth Extraction

For golden workflows (Layer 1), ground truth is computed from the AST:

```python
def compute_ground_truth(python_ir: str) -> dict:
    """Compute structural ground truth from Python AST."""
    import ast
    tree = ast.parse(python_ir)
    
    nodes = 0
    edges = 0
    decisions = 0
    
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.Assign, ast.Expr)):
            nodes += 1
        elif isinstance(node, (ast.If, ast.While, ast.For)):
            nodes += 1
            decisions += 1
        elif isinstance(node, ast.Break):
            edges += 1  # Loop exit edge
    
    # Edges estimated: each node (except exit) has at least 1 outgoing edge
    # Decision nodes have 2 outgoing edges (taken/not-taken)
    edges = nodes + decisions - 1  # Approximate
    
    return {"nodes": nodes, "edges": edges, "decisions": decisions}
```

### 3.3 Metrics

```python
async def experiment_e2_structural_accuracy(
    module02_endpoint: str,
    workflows: List[dict],
    output_file: str = "eval_data/results/e2_structural_accuracy.json"
) -> dict:
    """Measure V3 structural extraction accuracy against ground truth."""
    
    results = []
    node_matches = 0
    edge_matches = 0
    decision_matches = 0
    
    for wf in workflows:
        # Run verification
        cert = await _call_verify(module02_endpoint, wf["python_ir"])
        
        # Ground truth
        gt = compute_ground_truth(wf["python_ir"])
        
        # Extracted
        v3 = cert.get("v3", {})
        extracted = {
            "nodes": v3.get("nodes", 0),
            "edges": v3.get("edges", 0),
            "decisions": v3.get("decisions", 0)
        }
        
        # Compare
        result = {
            "uid": wf["uid"],
            "ground_truth": gt,
            "extracted": extracted,
            "node_match": abs(gt["nodes"] - extracted["nodes"]) <= 1,
            "edge_match": abs(gt["edges"] - extracted["edges"]) <= 1,
            "decision_match": abs(gt["decisions"] - extracted["decisions"]) <= 1,
        }
        results.append(result)
        
        if result["node_match"]: node_matches += 1
        if result["edge_match"]: edge_matches += 1
        if result["decision_match"]: decision_matches += 1
    
    n = len(workflows)
    
    metrics = {
        "experiment": "E2_Structural_Accuracy",
        "total_workflows": n,
        "node_match_rate": node_matches / n,
        "edge_match_rate": edge_matches / n,
        "decision_match_rate": decision_matches / n,
        "overall_match_rate": sum(
            1 for r in results 
            if r["node_match"] and r["edge_match"] and r["decision_match"]
        ) / n,
        "target_match_rate": 0.98,
        "target_met": (node_matches / n) >= 0.98,
        "per_workflow": results
    }
    
    with open(output_file, 'w') as f:
        json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2)
    
    return metrics
```

---

## 4. Experiment E3: Confidence Calibration

### 4.1 Purpose

Validate that the combined confidence score (1 - product of failures) actually correlates with correctness. A combined score of 0.99 should mean "almost certainly correct"; a score of 0.60 should mean "probably incorrect."

### 4.2 Protocol

```
For each workflow in Layer 1 (50 golden) + Layer 2 (100 augmented):
    1. Run through Module 02 /verify → get combined score
    2. Determine ground-truth correctness:
        - Golden (Layer 1): assumed correct (generated by LLM, syntactically valid)
        - Augmented (Layer 2): 
            - If preserves_semantics=True: equivalent to correct base
            - If preserves_semantics=False: extended (still correct, just more complex)
    3. Calculate correlation between combined score and correctness
```

### 4.3 Metrics

| Metric | Formula | Target |
|--------|---------|--------|
| Pearson correlation | `corrcoef(combined_scores, correctness_labels)` | r ≥ 0.85 |
| AUC-ROC | `roc_auc_score(correctness, combined_scores)` | ≥ 0.90 |
| Calibration (ECE) | Expected Calibration Error | ≤ 0.05 |
| Threshold accuracy at 0.95 | Fraction correctly classified at cutoff | ≥ 90% |

```python
async def experiment_e3_confidence_calibration(
    module02_endpoint: str,
    layer1_workflows: List[dict],
    layer2_workflows: List[dict],
    output_file: str = "eval_data/results/e3_calibration.json"
) -> dict:
    """
    Validate that combined certificate score correlates with correctness.
    """
    from scipy.stats import pearsonr
    from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score
    
    all_scores = []
    all_labels = []  # 1 = correct, 0 = incorrect
    
    # Layer 1: All golden workflows are assumed correct
    for wf in layer1_workflows:
        cert = await _call_verify(module02_endpoint, wf["python_ir"])
        combined = cert.get("combined", 0)
        all_scores.append(combined)
        all_labels.append(1)  # Correct
    
    # Layer 2: Augmented workflows — check if V3 extraction still works
    for wf in layer2_workflows:
        cert = await _call_verify(module02_endpoint, wf["augmented_code"])
        combined = cert.get("combined", 0)
        all_scores.append(combined)
        # Augmented code should also be structurally valid
        all_labels.append(1 if combined >= 0.95 else 0)
    
    # Calculate metrics
    pearson_r, p_value = pearsonr(all_scores, all_labels)
    
    try:
        auc = roc_auc_score(all_labels, all_scores)
    except ValueError:
        auc = None  # All labels same
    
    # Threshold accuracy
    predictions = [1 if s >= 0.95 else 0 for s in all_scores]
    threshold_acc = sum(p == l for p, l in zip(predictions, all_labels)) / len(all_labels)
    
    metrics = {
        "experiment": "E3_Confidence_Calibration",
        "total_samples": len(all_scores),
        "pearson_r": pearson_r,
        "pearson_p_value": p_value,
        "auc_roc": auc,
        "threshold_accuracy": threshold_acc,
        "target_pearson_r": 0.85,
        "target_auc": 0.90,
        "target_threshold_acc": 0.90,
        "pearson_target_met": abs(pearson_r) >= 0.85,
        "threshold_acc_target_met": threshold_acc >= 0.90
    }
    
    with open(output_file, 'w') as f:
        json.dump(metrics, f, indent=2)
    
    return metrics
```

---

## 5. Metric Target Table

The following metrics are defined in the research design and tracked across all experiments:

| Metric | Symbol | Definition | Target | Measured In |
|--------|--------|-----------|--------|-------------|
| Trace coverage | τ_cov | Fraction of WIR transitions exercised by differential testing | ≥ 0.95 | E1 (V1 pass rate on valid code) |
| Branch coverage | β_cov | Fraction of Python branches with verified WIR correspondence | ≥ 0.80 | E2 (decision match rate) |
| Mismatch rate | μ | Fraction of test inputs with α-trace mismatch | ≤ 0.01 | E1 (V1 mismatch on golden) |
| Refinement success | ρ | Fraction of critical transitions with proven simulation relation | ≥ 0.70 | E2 (V2 guard success rate) |
| Mutation detection | δ | Fraction of semantic-altering mutations detected | ≥ 0.95 | E1 (overall detection rate) |
| False positive | φ | Fraction of equivalent mutations wrongly rejected | ≤ 0.05 | E1 (BOR rejection rate) |
| Validation time | t_val | Wall-clock time per 100 LOC | < 300s | E1, E2, E3 (timestamps) |
| Combined-pass rate | π_pass | Fraction of valid workflows with combined ≥ 0.95 | ≥ 0.85 | E3 (threshold accuracy) |
| Structural accuracy | α_struct | Fraction with correct node/edge/decision counts | ≥ 0.98 | E2 (overall match rate) |

## 6. Running All Experiments

```python
# eval_data/run_experiments.py

async def run_all_experiments(module02_endpoint: str = "http://localhost:8000"):
    """
    Run all three experiments and produce a consolidated report.
    
    This is the main entry point for Phase 5. It:
    1. Loads all generated datasets
    2. Runs E1, E2, E3 sequentially
    3. Produces a consolidated results file
    4. Generates a human-readable summary
    """
    
    # Load datasets
    with open("eval_data/datasets/layer1_golden.json") as f:
        layer1 = json.load(f)
    with open("eval_data/datasets/layer2_augmented.json") as f:
        layer2 = json.load(f)
    with open("eval_data/datasets/layer4_adversarial.json") as f:
        layer4 = json.load(f)
    
    # Select 5 base workflows for E1
    e1_bases = layer1[:5]
    
    print("=" * 60)
    print("EXPERIMENT E1: Seeded Bug Detection")
    print("=" * 60)
    e1_results = await experiment_e1_seed_detection(
        module02_endpoint, e1_bases, n_mutations_per_type=20
    )
    print(f"Detection rate: {e1_results['overall_detection_rate']:.2%}")
    print(f"False positive rate: {e1_results['overall_false_positive_rate']:.2%}")
    print(f"Target met: {e1_results['detection_target_met']}")
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
    print("EXPERIMENT E2: Structural Accuracy")
    print("=" * 60)
    e2_workflows = layer1 + layer4
    e2_results = await experiment_e2_structural_accuracy(
        module02_endpoint, e2_workflows
    )
    print(f"Node match rate: {e2_results['node_match_rate']:.2%}")
    print(f"Edge match rate: {e2_results['edge_match_rate']:.2%}")
    print(f"Decision match rate: {e2_results['decision_match_rate']:.2%}")
    print(f"Target met: {e2_results['target_met']}")
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
    print("EXPERIMENT E3: Confidence Calibration")
    print("=" * 60)
    e3_results = await experiment_e3_confidence_calibration(
        module02_endpoint, layer1, layer2
    )
    print(f"Pearson r: {e3_results['pearson_r']:.4f}")
    print(f"Threshold accuracy: {e3_results['threshold_accuracy']:.2%}")
    print(f"Target met: {e3_results['threshold_acc_target_met']}")
    
    # Consolidated report
    consolidated = {
        "run_date": datetime.utcnow().isoformat(),
        "module02_endpoint": module02_endpoint,
        "experiments": {
            "e1_seeded_bug_detection": e1_results,
            "e2_structural_accuracy": e2_results,
            "e3_confidence_calibration": e3_results
        },
        "all_targets_met": all([
            e1_results["detection_target_met"],
            e1_results["fp_target_met"],
            e2_results["target_met"],
            e3_results["threshold_acc_target_met"]
        ])
    }
    
    with open("eval_data/results/consolidated_report.json", 'w') as f:
        json.dump(consolidated, f, indent=2)
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
    print("CONSOLIDATED REPORT")
    print("=" * 60)
    print(f"All targets met: {consolidated['all_targets_met']}")
    print(f"Report saved to: eval_data/results/consolidated_report.json")
    
    return consolidated
```

---

## 7. Calibration Procedure

If any target is not met, follow this calibration procedure:

### 7.1 Detection Rate < 95%

```
1. Identify under-performing operators from per_operator breakdown
2. For ROR/COR under-detection:
    → Increase V2 solver budget (BUDGET_TABLE queries += 50)
    → Check if Z3 timeout is occurring (increase timeout)
3. For STR under-detection:
    → Increase V1 test runs (n_runs: 50 → 100)
    → Check if reference interpreter handles the removed statement correctly
4. For BOR over-detection (false positives):
    → Tune V1 stutter elimination threshold
    → Check if branch swap changes trace ordering (expected behavior)
```

### 7.2 Structural Accuracy < 98%

```
1. Identify which adversarial cases fail (adv_00X)
2. For dominator failures (adv_001):
    → Check DominatorAnalyzer fallback path for disconnected graphs
3. For guard CNF failures (adv_002, adv_006):
    → Add test case to GuardExtractor for the specific pattern
4. For Python 3.10+ construct failures (adv_009 Match):
    → Verify CFGExtractor handles the construct (may need visitor addition)
```

### 7.3 Threshold Accuracy < 90%

```
1. Plot score distribution for passed vs failed workflows
2. If 0.95 threshold misclassifies many:
    → Adjust certification_threshold in ValidationConfig
    → Consider per-mode weighting instead of equal product-of-failures
3. If scores cluster (no separation between correct/incorrect):
    → V1 or V2 may be producing constant scores (check for saturation)
```

# Phase 6: Integration & Documentation

> **Phase**: 6 of 6  
> **Scope**: Module 03 API contract, thesis documentation, final integration checklist  
> **Prerequisite**: All Phases 1–5 complete

## 1. Module 03 API Contract

Module 02 must expose a stable, documented API for Module 03 (Bisimulation Checker) to consume. This section defines the contract.

### 1.1 Endpoint: `POST /verify` (Single Implementation)

**Maintained unchanged from existing implementation.** This is the core primitive.

**Request**:
```json
{
  "workflow_code": "def handle_incident() -> str:\n    incident = ServiceNow_incident__4_0_0__retrievewithwhere_incident()\n    if incident.impact == 'high':\n        issue = Jira_Issue__2_0_0__create_Issue()\n        message = Slack_message__3_0_0__create_message()\n        return 'high_impact'\n    else:\n        issue = GitHub_Issue__3_0_0__create_Issue()\n        return 'low_impact'",
  "specification": "Optional: BPMN XML or natural language"
}
```

**Response**:
```json
{
  "wir": {
    "entry": "node_0",
    "nodes": [
      {
        "id": "node_0",
        "type": "entry",
        "function": "handle_incident",
        "line": 1
      },
      {
        "id": "node_1",
        "type": "block",
        "statements": ["incident = ServiceNow_incident__4_0_0__retrievewithwhere_incident()"],
        "line": 2
      },
      {
        "id": "node_2",
        "type": "gateway",
        "guard_cnf": [{"op": "Eq", "left": "incident.impact", "right": "'high'"}],
        "line": 3
      },
      {
        "id": "node_3",
        "type": "block",
        "statements": ["issue = Jira_Issue__2_0_0__create_Issue()", "message = Slack_message__3_0_0__create_message()"],
        "line": 4
      },
      {
        "id": "node_4",
        "type": "block",
        "statements": ["return 'high_impact'"],
        "line": 6
      },
      {
        "id": "node_5",
        "type": "block",
        "statements": ["issue = GitHub_Issue__3_0_0__create_Issue()"],
        "line": 8
      },
      {
        "id": "node_6",
        "type": "block",
        "statements": ["return 'low_impact'"],
        "line": 9
      },
      {
        "id": "node_7",
        "type": "exit",
        "line": 9
      }
    ],
    "edges": [
      {"source": "node_0", "target": "node_1"},
      {"source": "node_1", "target": "node_2"},
      {"source": "node_2", "target": "node_3", "guard": true},
      {"source": "node_3", "target": "node_4"},
      {"source": "node_4", "target": "node_7"},
      {"source": "node_2", "target": "node_5", "guard": false},
      {"source": "node_5", "target": "node_6"},
      {"source": "node_6", "target": "node_7"}
    ],
    "control_variables": ["incident.impact"],
    "data_variables": ["incident", "issue", "message"]
  },
  "certificate": {
    "v1": {
      "score": 0.92,
      "tests_run": 50,
      "mismatches": 0,
      "input_entropy": 3.42
    },
    "v2": {
      "score": 0.85,
      "paths_explored": 12,
      "solver_queries": 87,
      "solver_time": 2.3,
      "loop_depth_reached": 2
    },
    "v3": {
      "score": 0.98,
      "nodes": 8,
      "edges": 10,
      "guards_cnf": 4,
      "branch_coverage": 1.0,
      "dominator_verified": true
    },
    "combined": 0.9997,
    "passed": true
  },
  "ai_refinement": {
    "counterexample_explanation": null,
    "narrative": "This workflow passed all three validation modes. V3 structural validation achieved a score of 0.98 with full branch coverage. V2 symbolic execution explored 12 paths in 2.3 seconds. V1 dynamic testing ran 50 inputs with zero mismatches. The combined certificate of 0.9997 significantly exceeds the 0.95 threshold.",
    "llm_used": true
  },
  "metadata": {
    "verification_time_ms": 4520,
    "module_version": "2.1.0",
    "timestamp": "2026-05-17T12:00:00Z"
  }
}

```json

  "wir": null,
  "certificate": {

    "v2": { "score": 0.20, "paths_explored": 2, "solver_time": 0.3 },

## 3. Module 03 Consumption Guide

Module 03 (Bisimulation Checker) should consume Module 02 outputs as follows:

### 3.1 Single-Implementation Mode

```python
import httpx

async def verify_and_lift(module02_endpoint: str, workflow_code: str, bpmn_spec: str):
    """
    Module 03 calls Module 02, then performs bisimulation checking.
    """
    # Step 1: Get verified WIR from Module 02
    async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:
        resp = await client.post(
            f"{module02_endpoint}/verify",
            json={"workflow_code": workflow_code}
        )
        result = resp.json()
    
    # Step 2: Check if verification passed
    if not result["certificate"]["passed"]:
        # Option A: Reject immediately
        return {"status": "rejected", "reason": "Module 02 verification failed"}
        
        # Option B: Use AI explanation for repair guidance
        explanation = result.get("ai_refinement", {}).get("counterexample_explanation")
        return {"status": "needs_repair", "guidance": explanation}
    
    # Step 3: Lift WIR to automaton for bisimulation checking
    wir = result["wir"]
    automaton = lift_wir_to_automaton(wir)
    
    # Step 4: Parse BPMN spec to automaton
    bpmn_automaton = parse_bpmn_to_automaton(bpmn_spec)
    
    # Step 5: Bisimulation check
    equivalent, witness = check_bisimulation(automaton, bpmn_automaton)
    
    return {
        "status": "verified" if equivalent else "divergent",
        "witness": witness,
        "module02_certificate": result["certificate"]
    }
```

### 3.2 Multi-Implementation Mode

```python
async def verify_batch_and_cluster(module02_endpoint: str, spec: str, n: int = 5):
    """
    Module 03 calls Module 02 in batch mode, then clusters by equivalence.
    """
    # Step 1: Get N validated WIRs
    async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:
        resp = await client.post(
            f"{module02_endpoint}/verify-batch",
            json={"specification": spec, "n_variants": n}
        )
        batch = resp.json()
    
    # Step 2: Filter passed implementations
    passed = [impl for impl in batch["implementations"] if impl["passed"]]
    
    if len(passed) == 0:
        return {"status": "all_failed", "implementations": batch["implementations"]}
    
    # Step 3: Cluster by bisimulation equivalence
    clusters = []
    for impl in passed:
        automaton = lift_wir_to_automaton(impl["wir"])
        
        # Check if equivalent to existing cluster
        assigned = False
        for cluster in clusters:
            equiv, _ = check_bisimulation(automaton, cluster["representative"])
            if equiv:
                cluster["members"].append(impl["variant_id"])
                assigned = True
                break
        
        if not assigned:
            clusters.append({
                "representative": automaton,
                "members": [impl["variant_id"]]
            })
    
    # Step 4: Select consensus (largest cluster)
    consensus = max(clusters, key=lambda c: len(c["members"]))
    
    return {
        "status": "clustered",
        "n_clusters": len(clusters),
        "cluster_sizes": [len(c["members"]) for c in clusters],
        "consensus_cluster": consensus["members"],
        "selected_variant": consensus["members"][0],
        "implementations": batch["implementations"]
    }
```

========================================
FILE: docs/module03/phase3_equivalence_engine.md
========================================
# Phase 3 Master Integration Plan: The Role C Equivalence Checking Engine

## Objective
To implement a dynamic, mathematically rigorous, and memory-safe Equivalence Checking Engine (Module 03) that eliminates the "Verification Gap." The engine will dynamically ingest WIR payloads, strictly manage C++/Python memory boundaries using the SPOT library, and execute an optimized divergence-sensitive stuttering bisimulation to evaluate whether AI-generated code ($M_{code}$) is observationally equivalent to a golden specification ($M_{spec}$).

## 1. Dynamic Input Mapping & Lifecycle Integration

To move away from the legacy static file paths and isolated script execution, the pipeline will adopt a robust ingestion and lifecycle model:

### 1.1 Shared Memory Context (`bdd_dict` Lifecycle)
- **Constraint:** Synchronous product intersection requires that boolean variables (e.g., transition guards like `loan_approved == true`) map to the exact same integer ID in both automata.
- **Solution:** A single `spot::bdd_dict_ptr` must be instantiated at the start of a verification batch. 
- **Pybind11 Integration:** The Python layer will control the high-level orchestration, but the C++ layer will firmly own the memory. We will utilize `py::smart_holder` and `return_value_policy::reference_internal` to guarantee that Python's garbage collector does not prematurely destroy the shared dictionary while `twa_graph` instances remain active.

### 1.2 Dynamic Ingestion & Resolution
- **Endpoint Structure:** The C++ `AdvancedLifter` will expose an `ingest_wir(json_payload)` method.
- **Spec Resolution:** Upon receiving the JSON payload, the engine extracts the `spec_id` from the payload header. It dynamically queries the local artifact store (or database) to retrieve the corresponding $M_{spec}$ WIR baseline. Both the spec and the generated code are then lifted into the shared memory context.

## 2. State Space Optimization & Scale Resilience

The legacy implementation (`repo_snapshot.txt`) relied on exhaustive, explicit matrices (e.g., `_compute_silent_closure` computing full reachability via BFS for every state), resulting in unacceptable $O(V^2)$ memory bloat. We will modernize this logic:

### 2.1 Tarjan SCC Pre-Collapse on the $\tau$-Graph
- Before refinement, the engine extracts the $\tau$-subgraph (transitions labeled as internal, noise, or `_`).
- We execute Tarjan's Strongly Connected Components algorithm explicitly on this subgraph.
- Any cycle composed entirely of $\tau$-edges is mathematically collapsed into a single macroscopic state. This prevents infinite stuttering loops from paralyzing the downstream bisimulation.

### 2.2 Shift to Groote & Vaandrager Partition Refinement
- **Algorithmic Shift:** We discard the explicit closure arrays. The engine will implement the Groote & Vaandrager algorithm ($O(m \log n)$ complexity).
- **Splitter Queues:** The engine initializes blocks based on observable labels. It maintains an active queue of "splitter blocks." A block is refined by calculating backwards $\tau$-reachability from the splitter. This dynamic, demand-driven splitting fundamentally mitigates the state-space explosion typically caused by dense sequential LLM logic.

## 3. Complete Boundary & Edge-Case Identification Matrix

The stochastic nature of Generative AI necessitates aggressive defensive programming. The engine will actively trap and flag the following anomalies during lifting and refinement:

1. **Unreachable Code:** 
   - *Detection:* Execute a topological BFS from the initial state ($S_0$) immediately after node parsing. 
   - *Action:* All states in the set $S \setminus \text{Reach}(S_0)$ are pruned before BDD allocation, preventing ghost-state explosion.

2. **Deadlocks (Premature Termination):**
   - *Detection:* Any state $s$ where $|Out(s)| = 0$ but $s \notin Terminal\_States$ (as defined by the BPMN end events).
   - *Action:* Tagged as `Premature_Termination_Error`. The pipeline rejects the automaton.

3. **Silent Divergence ($\tau$-Livelock):**
   - *Detection:* During Tarjan collapse, if a collapsed $\tau$-SCC possesses zero outgoing observable transitions (transitions with non-$\tau$ labels).
   - *Action:* The macro-state is isolated and flagged as a `Livelock_Violation`. The AI hallucinated an infinite loop with no business value.

4. **SMT Guard Conflicts (Dead Branches):**
   - *Detection:* During BDD edge creation, if the conjunction of a branch condition evaluates to `buddy.bdd_false()`.
   - *Action:* The transition is mathematically impossible. It is stripped from the LTS, and a diagnostic warning is logged regarding unreachable LLM logic.

## 4. E2E Production-Ready Verification Roadmap

This sequence ensures stable, incremental integration into the joint repository.

### Commit 1: Memory Foundation & The Shared BDD Lifter
- Refactor the core of `m_code_lifter.py` and `spot_lifter.py` into the C++ `AdvancedLifter` class.
- Implement the strict Pybind11 memory boundaries (`spot::bdd_dict_ptr` management).
- Build the dynamic JSON parser capable of instantiating `spot::twa_graph` instances utilizing the shared dictionary.

### Commit 2: Defensive Lifting & Topological Pruning
- Implement the unreachable code pruning (BFS from $S_0$).
- Implement deadlock detection and SMT guard conflict evaluation during edge construction.
- Integrate the semantic NLP label matching to differentiate observable actions from $\tau$-transitions.

### Commit 3: The Divergence-Sensitive Stuttering Engine
- Port the legacy `verify_determinism.py` logic natively to C++.
- Implement the Tarjan SCC $\tau$-collapse logic on the `twa_graph`.
- Implement the Groote & Vaandrager partition refinement loop utilizing dynamic splitter queues rather than exhaustive matrices.

### Commit 4: Clustering Pipeline & Output Layer
- Implement the deterministic graph serialization and SHA-256 hashing for the stabilized equivalence classes.
- Wrap the execution into the master clustering loop (`clustering_engine.py` equivalent).
- Expose the final verification boolean, the selected Representative Automaton (`LTS_rep`), and the cluster mappings back to Python for Module 04 UI consumption.
